                 2. A.    03.01.11 
2.       RECOGNITION 
A. Troy Foundation for Educational Excellence  
This evening we are pleased to recognize the Troy Foundation for 
Educational Excellence and the work they do to support our schools, 
our students and our staff. The Foundation awards $500 grants to help 
fund projects that support the curriculum in every school in the T roy 
School District. This fall, the Foundation received a record number of 
grant applications and was able to fund 77 grants submitted by 
teachers, parents and student groups.  
The Foundation is made up of volunteers who give of their time and 
talents to raise money and support innovative projects that enrich 
student learning.  
At this time, I would like to introduce  Mike Cianciolo, President of the 
Troy Foundation for Educational Excellence, who will briefly  recognize 
the most recent group of grant recipients and the many diverse ways 
they are bringing learning to life in our schools .

Schools Closed to Open 
Board of Education adopt a resolution to close Athens 
High School and Troy High School to open enrollment for 
the 2011/2012 school  year, with the exception of not 
more than 30 ninth grade students who live in the Troy 
High School attendance area but wish to attend Athens 
High School.  Students with siblings attending Athens 
High School and currently enrolled in grades 9 -11 will be 
allowed to open enroll to Athens for the 2011/2012 
school year.

WHEREAS, the Troy School District is one of a 
group of 467 school districts involved in two lawsuits 
against the State of Michigan seeking a declaratory 
judgment that the State is continuing to underfund 
school districts in Michigan for special education and 
other mandated services pursuant to Article IX, Section 
29 of the Michigan Constitution; and  
 WHEREAS, on May 12, 1998, the Board 
authorized participation of this School District in said 
suits subject to a cost limitation of $5,000; and  
 WHEREAS, on March 2 , 1999, this Board 
authorized participation of this School District in said 
litigation subject to a cost limitation of $10,000; and  
 WHEREAS, on June 20, 2000, this Board

authorized participation of this School District in said

 WHEREAS, suits are pending before the Michigan 
Supreme Court and awaiting decision on whether a 
declaratory judgment will be issued by the Michigan 
Court of Appeals; and 
 WHEREAS, there remains more legal services and 
associated costs to be incurred before the objecti ve of 
the litigation can be accomplished;  
 NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED  that this 
Board authorizes Secrest, Wardle, Lynch, Hampton, Truex 
& Morley to continue to represent the Troy School 
District in the pending suits and agrees to be responsible 
for its pro rata share of legal expenditures and costs, not 
to exceed an additional $5,000 beyond that which was 
previously authorized. 
Mr. Hauff asked at what point this litigation might end 
Comments 
and when would the legal fees be settled.   Mr. Rajter 
responded that  there has been discussion to settle and 
the District would see some monies reimbursed once the 
case is resolved. 
Mrs. Edmunds mentioned that this case was a result of 
the 1998 Durant lawsuit , which also cost a lot of money 
for litigation .  She also added  that we received 
approximately $5 – 6 million dollars from the Durant 
fund, which made it a good investment.   
Mr. Miletti asked how long the $5,000 would last.  Mr. 
Rajter stated that hopefully 18 months since most of the 
bills the District receives are from $300 to $400.

MEMORANDUM 
To: Mark Rajter, Assistant Superintendent, Business Services  
From: Scott Bryan, Director of Technology 
Date: February 22, 2011 
Re: SMART Interactive Whiteboards project purchase  
At the March 2, 2010 Board of Education meeting, the Board authorized the purchase of 100 Smart Boards 
to Smart Ed Services as the low bid response from a February 2010 bid  process; that equipment was installed 
during the summer of 2010.  The bid documents required the vendor to honor that volume discount pricing 
for one calendar year from the date of award.   I have negotiated a two week extension of the pricing to 
accommodate our Board meeting schedule.  
During the Smart Board project, many buildings have secured building internal account funds or PTO 
donations to purchase additional boards .  I therefore sent a “last chance” survey to buildings asking if they 
had plans to purchase additional boards prior to the expiration of the current bid pricing.   Bemis, Hill, 
Martell, Schroeder, Troy Union, and Wass elementaries, along with Athens, Troy High, and IA East, have all 
indicated that they have sufficient funds to acquire a total of 41 more Smart Board systems, for a total cost of 
$162,523.78.  These boards will be installed and teachers trained prior to the end of the school year.  
NOTE:  The Qomo document camera for secondary rooms  is less expensive from City Animation Inc. o n the 
statewide REMC contract than from Smart Ed and was not included in the original award.  Elementary 
schools do not require this piece of equipment because they already have document cameras and carts in 
their classrooms from a previous bond project.   Please see the attached spreadsheet for parts lists.   
All of these purchases are from either building funds or PTO sources ; none of them will be using bond 
funds.  Instead of a district-wide project, this is simply a collection of individual building purchases 
coordinated centrally for economy of scale, volume pricing, and consistency of installation.   
In summary, the requests for consideration are:  
 Approve the purchase of 23 Qomo document cameras from City Animation from the REMC contract 
in the amount of $22,747.00. 
 Approve the purchase of 41 Smart Board systems, including installation, from Smart Ed Services as 
an extension of the March 2, 2010 Board of Education award in the amount of $139,776.78.   
The total project cost will be $162,573.78 from building funding sources.  
This item requires Board action due to the dollar amount s of the purchases; please place this on the March 1, 
2011 agenda.  Thank you for your attention to this matter.

A. REPORT:  District Goals Update 
The District NCA Continuous Improvement Team’s mission is to 
facilitate the School Improvement process.   This team is made 
up of stakeholders from all parts of the school community.   The 
team continuously monitors Troy’s progress towards School 
Improvement goals.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  This evening’s report will be given by Kris 
Karbon.  Kris is Troy School District’s assessment coordinator and has been 
a member of the District Continuous Improvement Team since its inception 
about five years ago .  She will share with you the team’s findings and 
recommendations.

7.   CURRICULUM 
B.  CONSIDERATION:   Schools of Choice - Section 105 and 105c 
In accordance with Michigan state law, school districts shall pass a 
resolution each school year if they intend to participate in a Schools of 
Choice program.  It is anticipated that seats will be available for the 
2011-2012 school year for grades 6 - 8 at Baker Middle School.  It would 
be appropriate at this time to cons ider adopting a Schools of Choice 
Section 105 and Section 105c resolution for nonresident 6 th – 8 th grade 
students who attend another school district within Oakland County 
(Section 105) or if the student attends a district in another county, if that 
intermediate school district is cont iguous with the Oakland Intermediate 
School District boundaries (Section 105c).   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by: __________________________ 
 Supported by: __________________________ 
be it therefore resolved that the Board of Education of the Troy School 
District, exercising the option permitted by Section 105 and Section 105c 
of the State School Aid Act of 1979,  as amended by Public Act 300 of 
1996, will accept applications for a limited Schools of Choice program for 
the 2011-2012 school year for nonres ident students who attend another 
school district within Oakland Count y (Section 105) or if the student 
attends a district in another county, if that intermediate school district is 
contiguous with the Oakland Intermediate School District boundaries 
(Section 105c) for the purposes of a ttending grades 6 – 8 at Baker Middle 
School.   
Ayes: __________ 
Nays: __________

A. REPORT:  Budget Reduction Plan for 2011/2012 School Year 
Like all Michigan school districts, Troy has been struggling to balance its 
annual budget in the face of flat or insufficient revenue growth from the 
State of Michigan. The Board has been involved in several reviews of 
our district’s budget picture since t he beginning of this school year . 
Tonight, Mark Rajter , Assistant Superintendent, Business Services, will 
present a report on the budget reduction plan for the 20 11/2012 school 
year. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: 
This report will be given by Mark Rajter.  No action is required.

11.  CLOSED SESSION 
A. CONSIDERATION:  Negotiations 
The Board must pass a resolution to go into closed session. 
Following the passage of the resolution, the Board of Education will 
go into closed session .  No additional business will take place after 
this time, other than to officially adjourn the meeting.  
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
   Moved by  
   Supported by  
Be it therefore resolved  that, as permitted in the Michig an Open Meetings Act, 
Public Act 267, Section 8 (c), the Troy Board of Education shall go into closed 
session to discuss negotiations.  
Roll call vote.
